This is the round closing on the oldest thing in the game: HV-34's Writing on the Wall chalked the *bridge's* numbers where anyone could read them — now the newest person there writes their own name beside them.

- **~~LAB-58 · Grow Op advertised its strongest upgrade as its weakest~~** ✅ — An upgrade's description is a **promise the player spends money on**. Grow Op sells nine; **three did not say what the code did, and a fourth effect was not mentioned at all.** The worst by far: **Bigger Yield**, sold as *"+20% product per harvest per level"* for $200, while `bags = 1 + upgLv('yield1')` adds **a whole bag per level** — Lv1 is **+100%, not +20%**, a **5× understatement**, rising to +400% at Lv4. Read beside Premium Product's honest *"+40% for $500"*, any player doing the arithmetic buys Premium — and the strongest buy in the game sat there looking like the weakest. That is not a typo; that is a purchase made on bad information. Also wrong: **Lookout System** said *"20% slower per level"* where `Math.max(1, 6 - lv*1.5)` is **25% of base per level** (75% at Lv3), and **Clean Operation** said *"25% faster per level"* where `+ lv*0.5` is a **flat half-point of drain a second** — not a percentage at all, and on a bare operation Lv1 is +167%. **Better Grow Lights** also quietly raises `playerSpeed()` 5% a level and never said so. **The copy was corrected to the code, not the reverse** — the code is the balance this game has actually been played and tested on, and nerfing Bigger Yield 5× to match a wrong sentence would break every save in exchange for nothing. New suite **`tests/headless/promises.js`, 20 assertions**, which pins each sentence to the exact expression implementing it. **Static, and necessarily so**: Grow Op's game script is `<script type="module">`, so `UPG` and `salePrice()` are module-scoped and unreachable from `page.evaluate` — and the only way to reach them would be to bolt on a `window.__` hook, which is precisely what **QA-23's `nohooks.js` exists to forbid** after a leaked one shipped for four rounds. Reading the source is both the honest instrument and the better one here, since *"does the sentence match the coefficient"* is a source-level question. Non-vacuous: restoring the three original descriptions fails **7 assertions by name**. **Scope checked, not assumed.** All six flagships were examined for numeric purchase copy. Four state no percentages at all. **Voxel Isle makes five such promises and gets all five right** — `1.4` and `+6` for the Barn, `1.2` for the Farmhouse, `0.15` on a 60s cooldown for the Compost Heap, `1.25` for the Weather Vane, `DOVE_PREMIUM = 1.75` with a 90s window for the Dovecote — so its assertions are there to keep it that way, not to fix anything. **Grow Op was the outlier, not the tip of something.** One note on the suite itself: two of my own extraction regexes initially matched nothing (`animalGrowMult`'s trailing semicolon, and `compostT=(\d+)` catching the `let compostT=0` declaration instead of the cooldown), and **the "guards the guard" assertion caught both** — which is exactly the job it was added to do. **Battery now 128 suites.**

- **~~HV-56 · Homeless Village never told a new player anything~~** ✅ — Eighteen rounds went into **endgame** depth: prestige, standing effects, a sixteen-link memory chain, the panels that show it. Nobody had looked at the **opening** — the five minutes that decide whether a player ever reaches any of it. Measured on a fresh profile across all six flagships, five greet you and one does not. **Homeless Village put 47 live controls on screen** — the most of any flagship — under a goal reading *"Survive 3 days (0/3)"*, and said **not one word about how**. Two things a newcomer reliably bounces off are invisible from that screen: **scavenging is proximity-gated** (HV-2 — stood anywhere else the button just greys out, and the reason lives in a `title` tooltip, which is *nothing at all* on a touch screen), and **the Workbench gates 13 of the 18 recipes**, so miss it and most of the craft panel stays dark for no stated reason. Both are now stated, in a first-run panel matching the house pattern (Hearthvale's bulleted crash course, the Bridge's shell so the camp keeps one voice), reachable forever after from a **`?` in the top bar** — a crash course you can never re-read is a leaflet. **It also pauses the day**: HV stopped time only for game-over, so reading about the daylight would have cost you the daylight. New suite **`tests/headless/hvintro.js`, 15 assertions**. The half that earns its keep is the **drift guard**: the panel quotes real numbers (5 wood + 4 scraps, 13 of 18, warmth below 20%) and **quoted numbers rot** — I shipped exactly that bug in AOW-58, promising a 22-second Veteran bar the code set at 22.5. Every figure is now recomputed from `RECIPES` and the dawn code and compared against the rendered text; lying to a new player is worse than the silence it replaced, because silence makes them look and a wrong number makes them trust. Proved by planting AOW-58's mistake deliberately: editing the copy to "12 of 18" and "below 30%" fails **two assertions by name**. **Two honest notes.** First, my initial probe reported *Voxel Isle* as having no explainer too — that was **a probe artifact**, not a finding: its panel is positioned outside what my overlay filter matched, and it has a full crash course. Voxel Isle needed nothing. Second, the new panel made `reach.js` report **all 42 Homeless Village controls unreachable** — correct about the greeting, meaningless about the game, since it is modal on purpose. `reach.js` now arrives as a returning camp, and the coverage it gave up moved into `hvintro.js`, which asserts the controls are clear *once the greeting is dismissed* — a modal that failed to clear up after itself would lock a first-time player out of the entire game. **And it flushed out a latent test bug.** Pausing the day made `hvticket` fail — not because the pause is wrong, but because that suite's fare assertion was **silently dependent on the clock**. Section B sets `G.days = 20` to open the ask, which leaves the goal ladder owing *"Survive 3 days"* (+3 goodwill); `finishAction()` calls `buildActionUI()`, which checks the ladder. Whether that payout landed *inside* the measured window was decided by whether a background tick had already collected it. It passed for eighteen rounds on that accident and broke the instant the day stopped. Caught by trapping `log()` mid-assertion — the evidence was one line, `Goal complete: Survive 3 days. +3 goodwill.` The suite now settles the ladder before measuring, so it reads the fare and nothing else, and it is **green both with the pause and without it** — a fix, not a tuning. Same family as the `voxlantern` flat-3500 ms wait: *that was a fragile test, not a fragile lantern.* **Battery now 127 suites.**

- **~~QA-27 · The same half-a-guard one layer up, where it takes the whole arcade~~** ✅ — QA-26 fixed the per-game save. This is its sibling with a **wider blast radius**. A flagship's save belongs to that flagship: corrupt it and you lose one town. The hub's shared keys are read by the **front page** and by the **Hall of Fame** — the board carrying all six flagship records — so one bad write there is not one town, it is the way in to all of them. Probing found **13 crashes across three shared modules**, every one the identical mistake: **a `try/catch` around `JSON.parse`, and nothing checking the shape of what parsed.** `js/rivals.js` — `list()` returned whatever parsed; `null` parses fine and `Object.entries(null)` throws, while `[1,2,3]` and `"hi"` parse fine and yield entries whose `.s` is undefined, which `Object.keys()` throws on. Blank Hall of Fame. `js/telemetry.js` — `load()` checked `typeof s === 'object'`, which **an array passes**, and which says nothing about the three sub-maps (`launches`/`seconds`/`days`) that every reader indexes directly; a stored `[1,2,3]` blanked **the hub's front page**. `js/achievements.js` — `unlocked()` passed a stored `null` straight into `Object.keys()`; front page again. All three fixed at **the module's single read function** rather than at each caller, so a consumer added later inherits the guard instead of having to remember it — the same principle as QA-26's total `seasonIndexOf`. One deliberate choice worth naming: telemetry's repair **spreads the stored object first and overwrites only the dangerous fields**, rather than rebuilding from a whitelist. A whitelist would have silently deleted any field added later — *a data purge wearing a guard's coat* — and the point of a repair is to cost you the damaged part, not the book. New suite **`tests/headless/hubstore.js`, 14 assertions**, 5 shared keys × 2 views × 8 payloads. **Six of the eight payloads are valid JSON**, and that is the whole argument: the parse is not where this bug class lives. Non-vacuous — reverting the three module fixes turns 14/14 green into **5 failures naming all three modules and their payloads**. The suite also asserts *both* views, because the difference between them is the severity argument: `eureka-stats` and `arcade-achievements` fail on the **root**, not just the Hall of Fame, and losing the root is losing the arcade rather than a screen. **Battery now 126 suites.**

- **~~QA-26 · A corrupt save bricked three flagships, and nothing would have told us~~** ✅ — The battery guards geometry, leaked hooks, console errors, chain integrity and pacing. It did not guard **the one input every player supplies and nobody validates: their own save.** A save survives browser crashes, quota evictions, extensions, hand-edits and plain bit-rot, and it is read on the boot path — *before* any UI exists to say "that went wrong". A save that throws during load does not degrade the run; it takes the game away permanently, with no in-game way back. Probing it found **three real boot crashes on three flagships, all the same shape**: a number read out of the save, used as an array index, never checked for range. **Hearthvale** — `day:-1` made `SEASONS[Math.floor(-2/6) % 4]` into `SEASONS[-1]`, because **JS keeps the sign through `%`**; the resulting `undefined.name` threw in `updateHUD` on the first frame. **Voxel Isle** — the identical season formula, reached through a blanket `Object.assign(state, data.state)`, so `day:0` crashed it too. **Grow Op** — `goalIndex:-1` reached `GOALS[-1].label`. And reading the neighbours turned up a fourth: Hearthvale's `goalIndex` has a `>= GOALS.length` guard on the **top** of the index and nothing at all on the **bottom** — the same half-a-guard as Grow Op's. Fixed at two levels, because they fail differently: the season lookup is now **total** (`seasonIndexOf` lands in 0..3 for a negative, non-finite or non-numeric day), which is what stops 26 call sites in Hearthvale and 15 in Voxel Isle from each needing their own check; and every save-borne number that becomes an index or a rendered figure is **clamped at the load boundary**, so the game doesn't merely stop crashing while displaying "Day −999". The root habit being retired is `x || dflt`: it catches `0`/`NaN`/`undefined` and waves through `-1`, `Infinity` and `"lots"`. New suite **`tests/headless/saves.js`, 18 assertions**, asking two questions because they fail differently — garbage in the whole slot (7 malformed shapes), and hostile *numbers* in real fields (5 values each). The second found everything: the JSON parses, the shape is right, and one out-of-range integer walks into an array lookup. Non-vacuous in both directions — reverting the three fixes turns 18/18 green into **4 failures naming each crash and its payload**. It also **guards itself**: every row asserts the game *actually read the key*, instrumented on `Storage.getItem`. That check exists because the first draft of this probe used save keys I had **assumed rather than read out of the source** — three of five were wrong, and it came back perfectly clean, proving only that the games ignore keys nothing writes. A green run has to mean the payload was seen. The two flagships that **don't** have this bug avoid it structurally, and that is the pattern the other three should have copied: **Startup Tycoon recomputes its milestone indices from the restored totals at load** rather than restoring the indices themselves (`while (nextMilestoneIdx < MILESTONES.length && state.allTimeCash >= …)`), so there is no saved index to poison; and **Age of War** persists exactly one number and parses it as `Math.max(0, parseInt(raw, 10) || 0)` — a floor and a fallback, on one line. Derive an index from data you already validated, or clamp it where it enters. **Battery now 125 suites** (ALL SUITES GREEN on the full run).

- **~~QA-25 · How deep is the memory arc, actually? Forty-five sessions~~** ✅ — Eighteen rounds built a sixteen-link memory chain into each flagship, R48 made it visible to players, and **nobody had ever computed what walking it costs.** Every link is once-a-session and opens only after three uses of the one before it, so each link is a three-session tax on the next; links do run in parallel once open (each has its own latch, so a player does every open link every sitting), but that does not shorten the critical path. The answer is **45 sessions**, and it is the same 45 in all six games — the parallel shape held. **That number is a design choice, not a defect**: these are deliberately deep-endgame rewards, and R46's note already framed the chain as "a deep-endgame reward, not a faucet". Two things follow. First, it should be **known** rather than latent in six source files, so `tests/headless/pacing.js` computes and **prints it on every battery run**. Second, and this is the actual regression risk, it should stay a *choice*: one game quietly becoming twice as deep as its siblings is invisible from inside that game's own source, so the suite asserts the six stay in step on both link count and session depth. It also proves each chain is **completable at all** — every predecessor exists and comes earlier, which rules out a dangling gate and a cycle together. **28 assertions**, pure static analysis, no browser. Non-vacuous in both directions: raising one row's gate from 3 to 5 makes Voxel Isle 47 against its siblings' 45 and fails the in-step assertion by name; pointing a link at one that comes later fails the deadlock assertion naming the row. And the arithmetic itself is checked against a hand-computed three-link fixture, so the depth figure is not just plausible-looking output. **Worth putting to the owner rather than acting on unilaterally**: 45 sittings to reach the sixteenth link means the back half of the arc is, in practice, unreachable for most players. Halving the gate to 2 would bring it to 31, and gating on cumulative chain use rather than the immediate predecessor would bring it near 18 — but both are taste calls about what the arc is *for*, and rebalancing six games on my own judgement is not mine to make.

- **~~QA-24 · The audit could never fail the battery, and its "baseline" was 100% environment~~** ✅ — For many rounds I closed every tranche by reporting *"audit at its 12/32 baseline"* and treating that as a pass. Two things were wrong with it, and I only found them by finally **enumerating the twelve** instead of comparing the total. **(1) `tests/headless/audit.js` had no `process.exit`, so it always exited 0.** `run.sh` gates on `if ! node "$s.js"`, which means the audit has been in the battery for the entire project and **could never fail it**. Had a real page error appeared, the battery would still have printed ALL SUITES GREEN; the only thing standing between a regression and a green run was me reading a number by eye. **(2) All twelve issues are caused by this sandbox, not by any page** — 8 × `fonts.googleapis.com` `ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, 2 × `api.github.com` `ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID`, 2 × `localhost:3001` `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`. Both external hosts answer **200 to curl through the agent proxy**; it is headless Chromium that does not trust the proxy's CA. The localhost pair is `agentic-os.html` correctly probing for its local dev backend, which is not running here and is already suppressed as mixed content on the deployed https site. So the number could never go down — and worse, a real product issue *replacing* an environmental one would have passed silently at twelve. The audit now **classifies**: environmental failures are printed with the reason each is environmental and do not gate; the pass condition is **zero product issues**, which can actually be met and actually means something. The classifier is deliberately narrow — it is not "ignore network errors". A **same-origin** failure is always the site's problem, `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` counts as environmental only for the one documented local backend, and Chromium's bare console echo is matched on its exact wording, so anything with more to say than that is a product issue. Proved in both directions: the clean tree reports **0 product issues across 32 targets** and exits 0, and injecting a single `console.error` into `hearthvale.html` produces **`FAIL /hearthvale.html (1 product)`** and exit 1. **This changes how every future round should report**: "audit at its 12/32 baseline" was never the reassurance it sounded like, and the honest line is "0 product issues, 12 environmental".

- **~~HVALE-52 + VOX-50 · the Hall and the Shore — Round Forty-Eight closes~~** ✅ — The last two flagships, and with them **every one of the six can now be seen whole**: the Wall (Tycoon, beside Achievements), the Corner (Grow Op, in the tray), the Line (Age of War, beside Awards), the Bridge (Homeless Village, in the top bar), the Hall (Hearthvale, on the pause menu) and the Shore (Voxel Isle, in the tray). **115 assertions across six games, green** — one suite, one set of checks, six `GAMES` entries. Two notes. **(1) The dismissal step had to become a list:** a fresh Hearthvale opens on a full-screen welcome *and* keeps the Hall on the pause menu, so reaching the button takes two clicks before the one under test. `dismiss` now accepts an array and the field is documented as "everything in the way", which also future-proofs the harness against the next game that gates its UI behind an intro. **(2) The temporal-dead-zone trap bit for the FOURTH time**, on the last game of the round. Each time I had anchored the table "after the block I was thinking of" rather than after *all* the blocks it cites — a fix that works and a rule that doesn't. So the rule changed: **the table goes at the end of the script**, where it cannot be got wrong, and the comment now says that outright instead of naming particular blocks. Worth being blunt in the record: I wrote a NOTE ON PLACEMENT after the second occurrence, carried it into the third and fourth games, and still made the mistake — because the note described the *reason* rather than a placement a person can follow without thinking. A guard rail that requires judgement is not a guard rail.

- **~~HV-55 · Homeless Village: the Bridge — and a placement the guard rejected~~** ✅ — The fourth flagship's chain made visible, and the hardest shell so far: **Homeless Village had no modal of any kind**, so the overlay is built here rather than borrowed from a sibling. **77 assertions across four games, green.** `HV_CHAIN` is sixteen rows with the Bridge Reunion as the multi-predecessor row (the chalk star **and** Marisol's visits). Two things. **(1) The reachability guard rejected my first placement, correctly.** The button went in at `bottom:154px; right:10px`, mirroring `#craft-toggle` — but `#craft-toggle` is *mobile-only*, and on desktop that corner is the action panel (x 1210–1400, y 75–825). `reach.js` came back with **`stuck: action-anniv`** and **`stuck: action-marisol`**: two rows a player could no longer press. Rather than nudging the offset until the guard went quiet, I measured every panel at both sizes — left column 0–195 is craft and workers, right column is actions, top bar 0–51 and bottom bar 849–900 are the only strips that belong to nobody — and moved the button **into the top bar**, which cannot collide with a panel at any width and gets responsive behaviour for free. The CSS carries a comment saying exactly that, so the next person does not re-float it. reach back to 32/32. **(2) The static parser needed widening a third time**, and the three widenings together are the real lesson: `return(...)` with no space (Grow Op), a table indented inside an IIFE (Age of War), and now `var` instead of `const` (this game, a classic-script page). Each time it reported **0 rows and failed loudly** rather than silently passing, which is the one thing that makes an analyser like this safe to trust — but a check that only sees the shapes it was written against is a check with a blind spot, and it took four houses to find the fourth shape.

- **~~AOW-59 · Age of War: the Line — and a pause bug the surrounding code told me about~~** ✅ — The third flagship's chain made visible, and the first one to cost only a config block: the generalised `wall.js` took a **fourteen-line entry in its `GAMES` table** and nothing else, exactly as LAB-57 set out to make possible. **58 assertions across three games, green.** `AOW_CHAIN` is sixteen rows, with the Veterans' Reunion as the multi-predecessor row (the laurel **and** the Old General's visits). Three findings worth keeping. **(1) A real bug, found by reading the neighbours rather than by a test:** `anyModalOpen()` enumerates modal ids by hand, and the Line — which pauses the run like every other modal — was not in the list. Closing *any other* modal while the Line was open would therefore have unpaused the game behind a full-screen panel. Added, with a comment saying why the list has to grow when a modal does, and verified by driving it: open the Line, close the achievements modal, the Line is still up and the run is still paused. **(2) The static parser needed widening twice in two tranches** — Grow Op's `return(...)` last time, and this time the table itself, which lives *indented inside an IIFE*, so the closing-bracket pattern had to accept leading whitespace. **(3) The DOM wait had to stop assuming a convention:** Tycoon and Grow Op mark an open modal with a class, Age of War sets `style.display`. Rather than adding a per-game "how do I know it's open" selector, the wait is now for a **visible row inside the modal**, which is true of all three houses without caring how any of them does it. The `NOTE ON PLACEMENT` comment carried over and did its job — the table went in below the blocks it cites the first time, and the page loaded clean.

- **~~LAB-57 · Grow Op: the Corner — the same wall, on the block~~** ✅ — The second flagship gets its chain made visible, and the suite from TYC-59 was **generalised rather than copied**: `tests/headless/wall.js` now walks a `GAMES` table and applies the same eight checks per game, so the sixth flagship costs a config block rather than another suite. **39 assertions across both games, green.** `LAB_CHAIN` is sixteen rows on the same contract — every row calls the game's own loader, every gate is cross-checked against the game's own predicate, and the Block Reunion is the multi-predecessor row (the gold record **and** the OG's visits) that the Tycoon wall taught the table to expect. Three things worth recording. **(1) A real id collision, caught by pre-scan before it shipped:** `wall-toggle` is already the Wall of Names button on that corner, so the entire new family was renamed `chain-*` and the panel is called **The Corner** rather than The Wall — the duplicate-id guard in `reach.js` confirms the page is clean. My first pre-scan had missed it because I scanned the Tycoon names, not Grow Op's; the second scan covered both families. **(2) The temporal-dead-zone trap for the second time:** `LAB_CHAIN` cites `FRAME_AT` and was declared above the LAB-56 block that owns it, taking the page down on load exactly as `TYC_WALL` had. There is now a **NOTE ON PLACEMENT** comment at the head of the table saying so, since a future round moving this block would reintroduce it silently. **(3) The parser needed widening:** `gplaqueStands()` is written `return(...)` with no space, so the gate regex now accepts both houses' formatting — a reminder that a static analyser is only as good as the source shapes it has actually seen. The tray-count guard fired as designed (28 → 29, no geometry failure), and was bumped deliberately with its header note extended.

- **~~TYC-59 · Startup Tycoon: the Wall — Round Forty-Eight opens on a gap, not a feature~~** ✅ — Seventeen rounds of memory links have gone into this company and **there was no way for a player to see that they are one thing.** Each link's chip appears only once the link before it is done, so somebody eight deep has no idea there are eight more, what the next one is, or how close they are to it. The measurement: Tycoon alone carries **22 memory keys and 38 chips in the goal header**, and a grep for any "see the whole chain" view across the whole repo returns nothing. The chain was legible only from the source. `TYC_WALL` declares it once, in order — sixteen rows, each calling **the same function the game calls**, so no row can drift into quoting a tally the game doesn't keep — and each row states the gate that opens it as its predecessor plus a threshold. The 🧱 **The Wall** button sits beside Achievements, because it is the same question asked the other way: not what have I won, but what is this company remembering, and what is next. Rows read **done N times / yours to do next / N of 3 toward it**, and everything past the one you're on stays **unnamed** — the wall should say there is more, not hand over the list. **The drift guard caught a real error in my own table on its first run.** `reunionStands()` gates on *two* predecessors — the plaque's cheers **and** Employee #1's visits — and the row shipped naming only one. So `prev` now takes a list, a multi-predecessor row reports **the one furthest behind** (the thing actually holding it up, which is the only useful answer), and there is a dedicated assertion that satisfying either half alone does not open the Reunion. `tests/headless/wall.js` is **hook-free and in the battery**: the drift half is pure source analysis in the `storagekeys.js` mould (parse the table, find each gate function, read its loader and threshold, compare), and the rendering half seeds `localStorage` and reads the real DOM. **20 assertions**, including **three that prove the guard can fail** — a wrong threshold, a wrong predecessor, and a row naming only one of a two-condition gate are each caught with a named message. One shipping note: `TYC_WALL` first sat above the TYC-58 block and evaluated `YEARS_AT` in its temporal dead zone, taking the whole page down on load; the table now sits below it.

- **~~VOX-49 · Voxel Isle: the Bare Line — and the close of Round Forty-Seven~~** ✅ — The Long Shed was roofed with the far end left bare on purpose. Three hours in it and somebody drives a cleat into the boarding and hangs a fresh coil: **unknotted, uncut, nobody's hand in it yet**, for whoever ships out next season. What it does is quicken **every hand on the isle, forever** — a crew that never has to go looking for line never stands about waiting for it. `WORKER_SPEED()` gains **+0.2**, the same step the Tavern gives, `vox-bareline` as a `{up}` record carrying to every isle that rises after. The suite is **promoted into the battery as `voxline`** — **13 assertions, green first run**, and the one that matters most is the stacking, proved by driving all four corners of the truth table rather than by reading the source: **neither 1.9, tavern-only 2.1, line-only 2.1, both 2.3** — so the line demonstrably *adds* to the Tavern rather than overlapping it.

- **~~HVALE-51 · Hearthvale: the Fresh Plaster~~** ✅ — The Long Gallery's far end shipped as bare lime, waiting. Three sittings in, the plasterers float it and the painter primes it and **dates it forward** — panels for the winters nobody has survived yet, hung where every child in the valley walks past them. From then on **every villager born into the town arrives with 4 mastery XP already in hand**, a third of the way to Journeyman before they pick up a tool, in `hvale-plaster` as a `{up}` record carrying across every founding. Verified via a temp hook, **15 assertions, green first run** after two test-side fixes: the hook's spawn helper had to stand a cottage up out of thin air (a fresh valley has *zero* buildings, so `state.buildings.find(...)` returned nothing), and the seam step had to seed `hvale-mark` because `useGallery()` gates on three newcomers' panels and was refusing before it ever reached the sitting. Both were the test's setup, not the product's behaviour. The decisive assertion is again a behaviour: **two real villagers spawned through `spawnVillager()`, starting 0 XP and 4 XP** — and a third asserting both are still *Novices*, because a head start is not a rank.

- **~~HV-54 · Homeless Village: the Empty Hook~~** ✅ — The Dry Corner went up with a hook by the door and nothing on it. Three sits in, somebody puts a spare blanket on it and leaves it there. **No name on it.** It is for whoever comes in next, and it stays for whoever comes in after them. The effect takes the edge off **every dawn there is**: the season's own base warmth drain comes down 2, winter 18→16 and every other season 8→6, in `hv-emptyhook` with a `hook1` goal. The lever is chosen deliberately: it is the **one part of the cold HV-23's coat rack was written never to touch** — the rack blunts the weather's bite and the snap's extra and explicitly leaves the base alone, so the rack covers the bad mornings and the hook covers all of them, a little. The suite is **promoted into the battery as `hvhook`** — **13 assertions, green after one test-side fix** (the seam step had to seed `hv-mark`; `dryOffered()` gates on three names and the action was refusing before it reached the sit). Two assertions exist purely to prove the two mitigations stayed **disjoint**: the weather bites, the snap extra and the rack's cut are all exactly where they were, and a heat wave still *gives* warmth back rather than taking it.

- **~~AOW-58 · Age of War: the Blank Panels~~** ✅ — The Long Room's far end shipped unpainted — *"room left at the end"*. Three sittings in, the painters come back and prime it: not a panel of anything that happened, but **the frames for the musters that have not marched yet**, squared off and dated forward. It shortens the road to the stripe for **every soldier in every war from here** — Veteran at 22s instead of 25, Elite at 54 instead of 60 — in `aow-blankpanels`, outside the run, so no defeat takes it down. **The first draft of this shipped a bug and the test caught it.** Writing the effect as a tidy `× 0.9` multiplier put the Veteran bar at **22.5 seconds** — a threshold no player can see, count or reason about, and one that quietly made "22 seconds" in my own copy a lie. Two named assertions failed on it. The fix was not to round: it was to **state the two bars outright as integers**, so the code says what the game does. **17 assertions, green after that fix**, including four that pin each bar to the exact tick on both sides, and one that exists specifically to prove the hall's stripe-seeding (which writes `aliveT` at the *un-shortened* 25 and 60) still lands on Veteran and Elite once the bar moves — seeding *above* the bar is the point, and the panels only move the bar.

- **~~QA-23a · A temporary test hook shipped to production~~** ✅ — **The most serious defect any round has surfaced, and it was mine.** `window.__ldT` — a strip-before-commit hook written to verify LAB-53's walk-down — went out in commit `8108cad` and sat live on `drug-lab.html` through **four release rounds**. It exposed `setCash`, so the entire Grow Op economy was one console line away from anybody who opened devtools; it also re-exported `walkThemDown`, `saveDocent` and `saveMural`, which is every cross-run tally in that leg. The per-tranche pipeline *has* a strip step. The strip step is a human habit, and on that tranche the habit missed. Found by accident, while adding a hook to the same file for LAB-56 and noticing a `window.__` neighbour that should not have been there. **A full sweep of `origin/main` found exactly one** — every other round's hook was stripped correctly — but one was enough, and "we usually remember" is not a control. Removed, and the class closed with `tests/headless/nohooks.js`, **run first in the battery** because it needs no browser and should fail before anything spends thirty seconds booting Chromium. Five assertions: no `window.__<name>T` hook in shipped source; no `window.__` global at all beyond a **short allow-list that has to be edited on purpose** (`__hvReset`, Hearthvale's save-wipe escape hatch — it grants nothing and only does what New Game already does); no `debugger` statement; and **two that guard the guard** — that the scan reaches ≥20 files and that it reaches all six flagships *by name*, so a typo'd walk that matched nothing could never pass silently. `vendor/` is skipped wholesale: three.js sets its own `window.__THREE__` and that is not ours to police. Proved non-vacuous in both directions — reintroducing the exact leaked hook fails assertions A and B by name, and removing it passes 5/5.

- **~~LAB-56 · Grow Op: the Empty Frame~~** ✅ — The second leg of Round Forty-Seven, and the corner's first standing effect. The Back Room shipped with one thing deliberately unfinished: an empty frame past the new kid's panel, glass in it, nothing behind the glass. Three sits in, somebody hangs it properly and dates the back — **still empty, still for whoever's next**. Thirteen links deep and this is the first that doesn't pay: what it does instead is put the block on your side for good. Every face out there has been on that wall or knows somebody who is, and **undercover buyers come at 0.7× from the day it hangs, on every run, forever** — `growop-frame` as a `{up}` record, surviving New Identity like the room it hangs in. It multiplies into the *same* roll the burner phone already rides rather than replacing it, so a fresh burner under a hung frame reads the corner twice over. No control: it hangs itself on the third sit, because there is nothing to buy and nothing to decide. Verified via a temp hook, **12 assertions, green first run**, including **hanging it early being refused outright**, **the second hang being a no-op rather than a double discount**, **the burner stacking proved by arithmetic rather than by reading the source**, and **the sit that makes three hanging it with no separate control to find**. Hook stripped before commit — and this time the battery would have caught it if it hadn't been.

- **~~TYC-58 · Startup Tycoon: the Next Five Years — Round Forty-Seven opens~~** ✅ — Round Forty-Six ended with six paid rooms, and I wrote every one of them with the far end deliberately bare: *"room left at the end"*, *"bare lime plaster"*, *"bare boarding"*. This round fills them, and does it with the arc's first reward that **is not a purse**. Three visits into the Archive Wing, somebody fills the blank end — not with anything that happened, but with what the company means to do next: **the next five years pinned up in the same hand as the last five**, dated, initialled, hung where every interview walks past it. From that day every hire arrives having already read where this is going and starts **+3 XP** in, granted through the same `alumniXp()` seam the Alumni Network uses at both spawn sites, so it stacks with Founder U's campus perk and survives IPO like the wing it hangs in. `tyc-nextyears` as a `{up}` record; no chip and no control, because there is nothing to buy and nothing to choose. Verified via a temp hook, **13 assertions, green first run** — and the decisive one is not a constant but a behaviour: **two real hires spawned through `spawnIdeaWorker()`, one with the wall down and one with it up, starting 0 XP and 3 XP respectively.** Also proved: raising it early is refused, a second raise is a no-op rather than a double grant, and the visit that makes three fills the wall with no separate control to find.

- **~~VOX-48 · Voxel Isle: the Long Shed — and the close of Round Forty-Six~~** ✅ — The paid link on the water, and the sixth and last of the round. Three knots in three different hands and the marked line has run out of harbor wall: it ends at the ways and the newest knot is tied at the last inch of it. **350 🪙, once ever**, roofs the ways and runs the boarding straight out through them — the sunken chest up on trestles, the harbor log open on a stand, the shell wreath in out of the salt, every knot since the first mooring along one wall, and bare boarding at the far end. It grows nothing, moors nothing and sells nothing. The sit that follows is the deepest on the shore: **70 coins base + 5 per knot (cap 5, to +95)**, tallied in `vox-longshed` as a `{built, sits}` record. The 🛖 chip goes last into the `#vox-tray` that UI-2 introduced and does the round's double duty — `raise the shed for 350 🪙` while unbuilt (dimmed when the coin isn't there), `an hour in the shed for +85 🪙` once it stands, `sat` once used. Voxel Isle is hook-free, so the suite drives `useLongShed()` and the globals directly and is **promoted into the battery as `voxshed`** — **17 assertions, green first run**, including **a coin short refusing and dimming rather than lying about being ready**, **the exact 350 debit**, **the chip flipping from price to payout at the seam**, **the refusal to be bought twice**, and **the shed surviving a reload while the session latch does not**.

- **~~HVALE-50 · Hearthvale: the Long Gallery~~** ✅ — The paid link in the valley. Three newcomers' panels and the wall behind the hearth has run out of hall: the panels reach the buttress and stop, and the newest hand has nowhere to paint. **400 gold, once ever**, takes out the buttress for a roofed run off the hall that produces nothing, houses nobody and feeds no one — the founders' casket on its stand, the valley ledger under glass, every panel from the first winter to last week, and bare lime plaster at the far end. Sitting in it is the deepest visit in the valley: **70 gold base + 5 per panel (cap 5, to +95)**, tallied in `hvale-gallery` as a `{built, sits}` record. Hearthvale's memory links live on the pause menu rather than a HUD chip, so `useGallery()` carries the two stages in its refusals: an un-offered gallery, a short purse, and a second sitting each get their own toast. Verified via a temp hook, **13 assertions, green first run**, hook stripped before commit. One thing worth recording about the hook: it went in at the last `</script>` first, which put it *outside* the page's IIFE where `state` and `gallOffered` do not exist — the fix was to re-anchor it on `\n})();\n`, inside.

- **~~HV-53 · Homeless Village: the Dry Corner~~** ✅ — The paid link under the bridge, and the only one in the round whose price is not money. Twelve links down here and not one of them has ever asked for anything back — the wall, the notebook, the bench, the ballad, the coffee can, the fifth panel, the walk, the names. They are all chalk and paper under an open underpass, and every hard rain takes a little more of them. **12🧱 and 8📦**, once ever, sheet and roof the corner by the piling and carry everything the bridge remembers in under it. Nothing about it feeds anyone; what it does is stop the rain from editing the story. The sit that follows is the deepest under the bridge: **12 food base + 1 per name (cap 5, to +17)**, tallied in `hv-drycorner` as a `{built, sits}` record, with a `dry2` goal at two sittings. Homeless Village renders its own action rows, so this leg uses the game's existing dynamic-action pattern (`dryAvailable()` + `dryAction()`) rather than a static `ACTIONS` row: **one button whose label is `Roof the Dry Corner (12🧱 + 8📦)` while the corner is open to the sky and `Sit in the Dry Corner` the moment it has a roof**, with `buildActionUI()` called at the seam so the flip is visible immediately. The page is hook-free, so the suite drives `finishAction({id:'dry'})` directly and is **promoted into the battery as `hvdry`** — **17 assertions, green first run**, including **a scrap short and a sheet of cardboard short each refusing with no partial charge**, **the exact 12/8 debit**, **roofing not counting as sitting**, and **the roof surviving a reload while the latch does not**.

- **~~AOW-57 · Age of War: the Long Room~~** ✅ — The paid link on the battlefield. Three recruits have painted their own panel and the fresco has run out of wall: the panels turn the corner and stop at the armoury door. **900 gold out of the war chest, once ever**, takes the armoury wall out and runs the whole line through it — the muster roll off the door, the cornerstone cache up out of the yard, every panel from the first muster to last week, with room left at the end. It fires no shot and raises no rampart. The sitting that follows is the deepest on the field: **140 gold base + 70 per panel (cap 5, to +490)**, tallied in `aow-longroom` as a `{built, sits}` record. The 🏛️ action does the round's double duty via `useLongRoom()` — purchase while unbuilt, sitting once it stands — and the HUD refresh switches the chip from `900g` to `+350g` at the seam. Verified via a temp hook, **15 assertions, green first run** (the hook's `arm()` clears `gameOver`/`modalPaused`/`userPaused` because the page under test sits on the start screen; the run-state guard ships unchanged), hook stripped before commit. Two naming notes: `roll`/`ROLL_*` was already taken by AOW's Muster Roll, hence `LROOM_*`; and the reveal is written `style.display = 'inline-flex'` rather than the `''` the older links use, because `.aow-action` is `inline-flex` and an explicit value is what the general dead-reveal guard in `reach.js` is there to protect.

- **~~LAB-55 · Grow Op: the Back Room~~** ✅ — The paid link on the corner, in the corner's voice. Three tags in and the wall has outgrown the alley: **$20,000, cash, no lease**, takes the room behind the shop and puts the whole thing indoors — the polaroid, the wall of names, the shoebox, every panel — out of the weather and out of sight of anyone official. The money is gone either way; what it buys is the deepest sit on the block, **$140 base + $70 per tag (cap 5, to $490)**, tallied in `growop-backroom` as a `{built, sits}` record. The 🛋️ button reads as the purchase while unbought (dimmed when the cash isn't there) and becomes the sit once taken; it joins the tray and the refresh chain after `refreshMarkBtn()`, and **The Back Room** joins the Hall of Fame roster at three sits. Verified via a temp hook (12 assertions, green first run — the same shape as the Archive Wing including **a dollar short refusing and taking nothing**, **the exact $20,000 debit**, **the refusal to buy twice**, and **the room surviving a reload while the session latch does not**), hook stripped before commit, audit at its 12/32 baseline. Note the button needed no `right:` offset of its own: it goes straight into the `#lab-tray` that UI-1 introduced, which is the first new Grow Op control since that fix and the first that could not have been born off-screen.

- **~~TYC-57 · Startup Tycoon: the Archive Wing — the first memory link that costs something~~** ✅ — Round Forty-Six opens on a measurement rather than a theme. The memory arc now hands out **twelve capped once-a-session payouts in Tycoon totalling $4,075**, and the balance question was worth answering before adding a thirteenth: it is *not* broken, because each link gates on three uses of the previous one and each is once a session, so the full chain takes roughly **thirty sessions** to open — a deep-endgame reward, not a faucet. What the measurement did expose is a design gap: **eleven links of pure upside with no decision anywhere.** The Archive Wing is the first that asks for something back. After three painted panels the company can put up a wing to hold the whole wall — **$25,000, once ever**, and the money is gone whether or not you were saving it for a hire. What it buys is the deepest visit on the wall: **$150 base + $75 per panel (cap 5, to $525)**, tallied in `tyc-archive` (a `{built, visits}` record rather than a bare counter, so the purchase and the tally persist independently). The 🗄️ chip does double duty — it reads `wing $25k` and dims when unaffordable, then becomes the visit once built. Verified via a temp hook, **12 assertions**: the constants and ach/chip registered, two panels offering nothing, **a dollar short refusing and taking nothing**, the purchase debiting exactly $25,000, **refusing to be bought twice**, the purse scaling $375→$525 with the cap-5 enforced, the visit paying and ticking, the same-session refusal, the rearmed pay, three visits crowning the ach, and **the wing surviving a reload while the session latch does not**. That last one failed on the first run and the fault was the test, not the game: `addInitScript` re-runs on reload and was wiping the very keys the assertion existed to check, so it now clears once behind a `sessionStorage` guard. Hook stripped before commit; audit at its 12/32 baseline.

- **~~QA · three clean audits, and two of them become permanent guards~~** ✅ — After the dead-reveal class was closed, three further probes came back **clean**, and they are recorded here because a negative result is worth as much as a finding when it says which classes of bug the codebase does *not* have. **(1) Dead element handles.** Every `getElementById`/`querySelector('#…')` across the seven pages, checked against the live DOM: 254 ids referenced in Tycoon alone, and after adjudication **zero** genuinely dead. The raw probe flagged 69 across five pages, but every one is created at runtime — most as markup in a template, and four (`aow-council`, `tyc-fatal`, `hv-fatal`, `trade-order`) via `el.id = '…'`, a creation form my first detector didn't recognise and mistakenly reported. **(2) localStorage symmetry.** Every literal key each game writes, it also reads, and vice versa — **139 keys, no orphans**, so nothing the memory arc stores is written into a void. **(3) Cross-game key collisions.** All six flagships and the hub share one origin and therefore one localStorage namespace; across **140 distinct keys, none is claimed by two games**. Two of the three are now enforced rather than merely observed: `tests/headless/storagekeys.js` (15 assertions, pure static analysis, no browser) is promoted into the committed battery — **116 → 117 suites**. **The guard was verified by injecting a one-character typo** (writer `growop-marks`, reader `growop-mark`): it fails naming both sides, then passes once reverted. This matters because the memory arc has added roughly twenty keys over recent rounds and will keep adding them, and a key mismatch is invisible to every other suite — save and load round-trip fine inside a single page session, so only a cross-check of the sources catches it. This closes the structural-audit line: after eight real defects the last three probes found nothing, which is the point at which continuing costs more than it returns.

- **~~TYC-BUG-3 · the prestige season badge never appeared either — same defect, found by generalising it~~** ✅ — Having found one dead reveal, the useful question was *how many are there*. A repo sweep found **79** `style.display = ''` sites; almost all are legitimate (they clear an inline `display:none` the same code set). The dangerous case is only when the **stylesheet** hides the element. Detected empirically — for every element with an id, no inline display yet computed `none`, the stylesheet is doing the hiding — then cross-referenced against which of those ids any code reveals with `''`. Across all six flagships the intersection was **exactly two**: `#ipo-btn`, already fixed, and **`#season-badge`**, hidden by `#season-badge { display: none }` and revealed with `''` at two sites (on IPO, and on load when `prestigeLevel > 0`) — so **the S1/S2 prestige badge never appeared** either. Fixed to `'inline-block'` at both sites; measured `none`/0-box → a rendered 31×16 badge in the status row. Three near-misses were checked and **cleared**: `#add-desk-btn` and `#funding-btn` are hidden by `.panel.collapsed > :not(.panel-title)` — correct behaviour inside a collapsed panel, not a dead reveal — and `#investor-banner` (`[hidden]` attribute) and `.ipo-first-hint` (class-toggled) are not `''` targets at all.

- **~~QA · `reach` grows to 32 assertions, and the new guard is proved to fail~~** ✅ — Rather than pinning the two known ids, the suite now checks **the anti-pattern itself**: for each flagship it fetches the source, maps `getElementById` variables to ids, finds every `style.display = ''` reveal, and asserts that none of those elements is hidden by a rule targeting the element itself. Ancestor rules are deliberately excluded so a collapsed panel doesn't read as a bug. **The guard was verified by reverting both fixes: it fails and names them — `— ipo-btn, season-badge` — then passes again once restored.** Worth recording that the three IPO-specific assertions added earlier did *not* fail on that revert: they set the display value themselves, so they test revealability and geometry rather than the shipped code. The general guard is the one that actually pins the fix. Battery: **`ALL SUITES GREEN (116 suites)`**.

- **~~TYC-BUG-1 · the IPO button never appeared, so prestige was unreachable~~** ✅ — **The most serious bug the audit has found, and it is not cosmetic.** `#ipo-btn` is hidden by its **stylesheet** rule (`display: none` sits in the `#ipo-btn { … }` block; the markup carries no inline style). `checkIPOAvailable()` revealed it with `ipoBtnEl.style.display = ''` — which clears the *inline* property and lets the stylesheet's `none` apply again. **The button therefore never appeared, at any cash level.** And it is the sole entry point: `openIPOModal` has exactly one caller, `ipoBtnEl.addEventListener('click', openIPOModal)`, so **IPO — the prestige mechanic, the permanent +2× revenue multiplier, the gateway the whole Founder Shop hangs off — could not be reached by any player.** The Insights "next best step" card can't rescue it either: that path guards on `ipoBtn.offsetParent !== null`, which is null while the element is `display:none`. Proved in the browser rather than argued from the source — with the shipped line, computed display stays `none` and the box measures **0×0**; with a real value it becomes `block`, **100×44**, and `elementFromPoint` at its centre answers `ipo-btn`. Fixed to `display = 'block'`. **Provenance: not from this session** — `git log -S` puts both the CSS `display:none` and the `''` assignment in `dccd0ce` (#213), many rounds before this work began.

- **~~TYC-BUG-2 · fixing the reveal exposed a collision underneath it~~** ✅ — `#ipo-btn` sits at `bottom:18px;right:18px` and `#tp-toggle` sat at `bottom:16px;right:16px` — **the same corner, 2px apart**, with the toggle carrying `z-index:120` against the button's auto. Invisible for as long as the IPO button was, and a direct cover-up the moment it worked. The toggle clears to `bottom:76px` on desktop (the button occupies 18–63). Re-measured: no overlap, and both own their own centres. This is the second time in this audit that a fix has *revealed* the next defect rather than completing the job — the same thing happened raising the toggle over Tycoon's Sprint button in UI-5 — which is the argument for re-measuring after every fix instead of trusting that one change closed the case.

- **~~QA · `reach` grows to 26 assertions~~** ✅ — Three new ones pin the IPO bug specifically: that the button really is stylesheet-hidden (so clearing an inline style *cannot* reveal it — the assertion documents why `''` was wrong), that a real display value yields a visible button owning its own centre, and that the revealed button does not collide with the camera toggle. Battery re-run: **`ALL SUITES GREEN (116 suites)`**.

- **~~UI-5 · two flagships had controls that could not be clicked at their own centres~~** ✅ — A different probe from UI-1's: instead of asking *is this box inside the viewport*, ask **`document.elementFromPoint` at each visible control's centre and see whether the control is what answers**. Two real defects, both invisible to every behavioural suite because the handlers were wired correctly the whole time — only the pixels were wrong. **Startup Tycoon's phone corner was stacked by eye rather than by arithmetic**: `#next-action-card` is full-width at `bottom:78px` and 56px tall, so it owns the band 78–134 and carries `z-index:95`; `#sprint-btn` sat at `bottom:72px` (72–106), *inside that band and underneath it*, with `#tp-toggle` (`z-index:120`, 88–123) over the top of both. **Sprint was not clickable at its own centre on a phone.** The column is laid out by measurement now — card 78–134, sprint 142–176, toggle 184–219 — and raising the toggle alone was explicitly *not* enough: it just exposed the card underneath, which the re-measure caught before shipping. **Voxel Isle pinned `#topbar` and `#back` at `top:10px` each**, and at 390px the chip bar (x 10–383) and the nav cluster (x 154–380) overlapped, so **five nav buttons — first-person, farmer, animals, town, almanac — lost their centres to the chips**. `#back` drops to its own row beneath the chips and spans the full width, wrapping into two rows instead of three. Re-measured: **both games clean at 390×844 and 1400×900.**

- **~~UI-5n · the Tycoon desktop "finding" was a probe artifact, and is not a bug~~** ✅ — Worth recording as a negative result. An earlier sweep reported `upg-btn` covered by `CANVAS` on Tycoon at 1400×900, and it reproduced intermittently. Chased with a scroll-aware probe over three clean runs: all fourteen hits report **`rect=0,0 0x0`**. They are buttons inside *collapsed* sidebar sections — not rendered, so `elementFromPoint` names the canvas behind them. Nothing is covered and nothing was changed. The `reach` suite now skips zero-sized elements explicitly so this cannot come back as a false alarm.

- **~~QA · `reach` grows to 23 assertions~~** ✅ — Adds centre-coverage checks for Startup Tycoon and Voxel Isle at phone and desktop sizes, with the zero-size guard above. Battery re-run: **`ALL SUITES GREEN (116 suites)`**.

- **~~UI-1b · the tray regressed mobile, and the hit test caught it~~** ✅ — UI-1's tray stripped every button's per-button mobile offset — and those offsets had been **hand-placed to clear `#manage-btn`**, a `bottom:14px;right:14px` control that only appears at ≤700px. Verified by probing the pre-UI-1 commit in a second worktree served side by side: **before, no covered buttons; after, `crunch-toggle` sat under `manage-btn`.** Fixed by lifting the tray above it at that same breakpoint (`bottom:62px`, `max-height:calc(100vh - 76px)`), re-verified against both trees: no covered buttons on either. Recorded because the regression was mine, shipped an hour earlier in #628, and found by the very technique that motivated it.

- **~~UI-3 · Homeless Village: the side panels never learned to scroll~~** ✅ — Same root cause as the Grow Op ladder, a different geometry. Both columns were laid out as *centred, height = whatever the content is*. That was fine when the lists were short; **seven rounds of new `ACTIONS` rows later `#action-list` measured 1035px tall in a 900px viewport**, so the last actions sat under `#bottom-bar` unclickable, and `#craft-panel` had grown down over `#workers-panel` far enough to bury five craft buttons. Nothing scrolled because nothing was ever bounded. `#craft-panel` is now pinned between the top HUD and the community panel (`top:64px;bottom:230px`) instead of centred, `#workers-panel` capped at 140px, `#action-panel` bounded to `calc(100vh - 150px)` — all three scrolling, all three scoped to `@media (min-width:701px)` so the existing mobile treatment (horizontal action strip, craft bottom-sheet) is untouched. Measured with a **scroll-aware** reachability probe — a control counts as reachable if it is hittable, or becomes hittable once scrolled into its own container — Homeless Village went from **10 unreachable controls to 2**, and both survivors are false positives: `pack-up-btn` is a zero-sized sweep-only control and `ev-close` is not in the DOM.

- **~~UI-4 · Homeless Village had two elements with `id="action-panel"`~~** ✅ — **A bug I shipped in HV-50 and did not catch, because the pre-scan looked at the wrong namespace.** Action buttons are built as `id="action-" + a.id`; HV-50's action id was `panel`, so its button rendered as `<button id="action-panel">` — colliding with the `#action-panel` container div that has existed since the game was written. Invalid HTML, `getElementById` returns only the first, and **the Fifth Panel button had been silently inheriting the panel container's `position:absolute;right:0;top:50%;width:190px` styling from the day it shipped**. My HV-50 identifier pre-scan checked `id:'panel'` against the JS and found it clean; what it never checked was the *DOM id the action would generate*. Fixed by renaming the action id `panel` → `fifth` (config row, player branch, suite refs); the storage key `hv-mural`, the goal id `panel2` and every function name are untouched, so no save migrates. A repo-wide sweep confirmed this was the **only** such collision across all seven pages.

- **~~QA · `reach` grows to 19 assertions~~** ✅ — The suite now also asserts **no duplicate element ids on any of the seven pages** and **every Homeless Village action and craft button reachable** at two desktop sizes, scroll-aware. Battery re-run after the action-id rename and the layout change: **`ALL SUITES GREEN (116 suites)`**.

- **~~UI-1 · Grow Op: 18 of 27 controls were off the screen~~** ✅ — **A real, shipped bug, found by measuring instead of asserting.** Every one of Grow Op's 27 controls was its own `position:fixed` element on a horizontal ladder, each new round appending another rung 128px further left: `right:364px`, `540px`, … `3776px`, `3904px`. Measured in headless Chromium with each element forced visible: at **1400×900, 18 of 27 sat off the left edge** (`mark-toggle` at `left=-2553`); at **1920×1080, 15**; at **2560×1440, 11**. The `@media (max-width:640px)` block was the only thing that ever rescued them, so the bug was invisible on a phone and total on every desktop — the Lawyer, Pre-Sale, Youth Center, High-Water Mark, Corner Ledger, Zippo, OG, Reunion, Polaroid, Anniversary, Wall of Names, Stoop, Stoop Story, Block Track, Shoebox, Mural, Walk-Down and Tag were all unclickable on a laptop. The fix retires the ladder: all 27 buttons move into one `#lab-tray`, a `flex-wrap:wrap-reverse` container anchored bottom-right with `max-width:calc(100vw - 16px)` and `overflow-y:auto`, and the pinning declaration is stripped from all 27 CSS rules and all 27 mobile media queries (which keep their font/padding shrink). Re-measured: **27/27 reachable at every viewport tested**, tray wrapping to 3 rows, page never scrolling sideways.

- **~~UI-2 · Voxel Isle: the chip ladder clipped off the top~~** ✅ — The same failure mode, milder and vertical: 19 HUD chips each `position:fixed` climbing to `bottom:698px`. At **1400×720 one chip clipped off the top**; at **650 tall, three did**. Fixed the same way — one `#vox-tray`, `flex-direction:column-reverse`, `max-height:calc(100vh - 28px)`, `overflow-y:auto` — with the chips **re-emitted in ascending order of the `bottom` offset each used to carry**, so `column-reverse` reproduces the exact stack players see today rather than reshuffling it. Re-measured: **0 clipped at 900, 800, 720 and 650**.

- **~~QA · the `reach` suite: geometry gets a regression test~~** ✅ — This class of bug survived many rounds because **nothing measured geometry**. Every other suite asserts behaviour through the DOM, and the DOM is perfectly happy to report a button as present, styled and clickable while it sits at `left:-2553`. `tests/headless/reach.js` (10 assertions) forces every control visible and measures its box against the viewport across four Grow Op viewports and four Voxel Isle heights, and asserts the trays reflow rather than overflow. Promoted straight into the committed battery, which grows **115 → 116 suites**; the tests README gains a *Cross-game geometry* row. Full battery re-run after the restructure: **`ALL SUITES GREEN (116 suites)`, zero failures** — two flagship pages restructured, nothing behavioural disturbed.

- **~~SITE · Round Forty-Five wrap: the marks ship (QA-22)~~** ✅ — **Flagship Depth 324.** The service worker steps to **`eureka-v53`** so installed PWAs pick up Depths 318–323 (the six marks: new hire's panel, new kid's tag, recruit's panel, name on the wall, newcomer's panel, their own knot) plus these notes; the hub's **patch-notes modal** leads with *Round Forty-Five*; the committed battery grows **113 → 115 suites** (`hvmark` and `voxmark` promoted — the four hook-based mark suites stay one-shot by design, their hooks stripped before commit); the tests README rows extend to match; the SW precache list re-validated at **71/71 paths resolving**. **QA-22: the full battery ran clean — `ALL SUITES GREEN (115 suites)`, first pass, zero flakes**, the PWA suite confirming the offline shell, the 3D boot from cache and the clean-URL fallback all still hold under the new service-worker version. **This wrap closes the memory arc.** It began at Round Thirty-Eight with a bench somebody sat on and ran: bench → story → song → capsule → mural → docent → mark. Each round added something the place *keeps*; this last one hands the brush, the can, the chalk and the line to whoever just arrived, and the wall stops being a thing the place keeps and becomes a thing it grows. Every one of the six is stocked entirely from earlier rounds' artifacts — nothing was invented to fill them. Two design constraints shaped the round rather than being papered over: Homeless Village writes its mark on **HV-34's Writing on the Wall**, the oldest surface in that game, and Voxel Isle — the one flagship with no wall — ties **a knot into the boarding** instead, which is how every other artifact in that game got there too. Per the every-other-round cadence, the next full regression (QA-23) lands at the Round Forty-Seven wrap.

- **~~TYC-35 · Startup Tycoon: the Endowment~~** ✅ — **Round Twenty-Four opens: the legacy round**, and it is the financial arc's deliberate coda. Five rounds taught the games to earn, store, gamble, insure and borrow; this round spends the fortune on the one thing a balance sheet can't hold — **permanent public goods denominated in each game's SOFT currency**. The Tycoon leg endows the Founders' Scholarship: **$25,000, once ever (a 🎓 chip beside the factor's, post-IPO), and the endowed office never despairs — no morale blow lands below 50, forever**. The floor lives inside `changeMorale` itself, catching both the team-wide and single-worker paths, so every despair source in the game — a lost race, a bugged launch, a War Room crash, a poached senior — hits the same cushion; each steadied day is counted and fed ("the scholarship steadied the office"), and the floor deliberately lifts anyone a pre-endowment save left beneath it, because that is the fund's point. Positive deltas and above-floor blows are untouched — the endowment removes despair, not consequence. `endow` rides its own SAVE_SCHEMA entry ({done, catches}); 🏆 **In Perpetuity** crowns an endowed office through five steadied days. Verified one-shot via a temporary hook (12 assertions: the 25000/50 constants + registration, the garage-hidden chip, a PRE-endowment blow landing straight through 50, a funded purchase at exactly −$25,000 standing once-ever with the double-buy refused, an endowed blow landing at exactly 50 with the catch counted, an above-floor blow landing normally, a raise untouched, the five-catch crown, fund + tally riding the save, legacy migration), hook stripped, audit at its 12/32 baseline.

- **~~VOX-25 · Voxel Isle: the Trader's Note~~** ✅ — **Round Twenty-Three's flagship lap is complete: six advances against six futures, and every debt is repaid by the exact income stream it was borrowed against.** The isle's leg is a lien on trade: a new 📝 chip (visible only with a 🛒 Trader's Market standing) signs **the Trader's Note — 60 🪙 wired now against 75 of the isle's FUTURE TRADE, with half of every Market sale and Tavern meal riding the note until it clears**. The scoping is the design: farm income, festival tips, truffles and balloon fares stay whole — `garnishTrade(n)` wires into exactly the two seams that ARE the Trader's lane (`sellStockpile`'s bulk buy and `serveMeal`'s tavern service), so the debt lives where the creditor does. The round's ledger, closed: the Tycoon factored a minute of revenue, the corner pre-sold five bags off the line, the wall borrowed against the field's coins, the camp against five quiet mornings, the valley against the harvest wagon, and the isle against its own market stall — **credit, in every game, is a claim on the future the game already simulates**. One note at a time; `traderNote`/`notesCleared` ride the whole-state save for free; 🏆 **Square with the Trader** crowns three cleared notes; the how-to gains its bullet. Verified one-shot with no hook needed (12 assertions: the 60/75 constants + registration + bullet, the market-gated chip and no-market refusal, a signing at exactly +60 with 75 booked, the one-note refusal, the half-garnish (30 in → 15 kept), the capped clear with the count ticking, free trade passing whole after, **a REAL `sellStockpile` sale settling the note end-to-end**, the three-clear crown, the ledger riding the save, legacy migration), audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~HVALE-27 · Hearthvale: the Merchant's Advance~~** ✅ — the credit round at the market stall, and it is the first debt in the arc collateralized by REPUTATION: **a new 📜 button on the Market's panel takes the merchant's advance — 30 gold NOW against 12 food due at his wagon in four days**. The dawn of the due day settles it inside the same chain as everything the valley owes the calendar (`advanceAtDawn()` right after `iceAtDawn()`): **an honoured note takes exactly its 12 food and pays +2 cheer — the town's word holding is worth something — while a short larder surrenders what's there plus 3 gold a measure and −3 cheer, the shame of a broken word**. The strategy is the farm calendar itself: take the advance in early autumn against the harvest coming in and it's nearly free money; take it in deep winter against a thin larder and the wagon will collect the difference. One note at a time; a standing note (`merchantAdvance` with its due day) rides the explicit save lists with migration ("older stalls paid on delivery"); `advancesFilled` books the record; 🏆 **Good for It** crowns three honoured notes. Verified one-shot via a temporary hook (11 assertions: the 30/12/4/3 constants + registration, the fresh empty book, an advance paying exactly +30 with the due day booked, the one-note refusal, a pre-due dawn passing without collecting, an honoured due day taking exactly 12 food at +2 cheer with the keep counted and the Chronicle receipt, a short larder paying 5 food + 21 gold at −3 cheer with no keep, the three-keep crown, a standing note riding the save, legacy migration), hook stripped, audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~HV-30 · Homeless Village: the Borrowed Favor~~** ✅ — the credit round comes to the fire, and it is the arc's gentlest instrument because the lender is a friend: **a new 🤲 action on the bar has Old Ray front 4 goodwill on the spot, and his ledger takes ONE back every dawn until 5 is repaid — the extra one is for the trouble**. The character note is the mechanic: **Ray never presses a broke morning** — a dawn with zero goodwill collects nothing and the debt simply holds, no penalty spiral, no compounding — **he just remembers**, and the only hard rule is one standing loan at a time. The repayment rides the same dawn seam as every settling thing in the camp (right after the rain bet's, before the snap rolls), so a borrowed cushion before a hard stretch is real strategy: 4 goodwill tonight against five quiet mornings is exactly the trade a camp low on soup-night funds needs. Borrowing bumps Ray's friendship like any visit; `rayDebt`/`rayLoans` ride the whole-object save with migrations ("saves from before HV-30"); the goal ladder gains **borrow2** (repay two loans in full). Verified one-shot with no hook needed (10 assertions: the action + goal + 4/5 slate, a front at exactly +4 booking 5 owed — survive-goal payouts primed first — the mid-debt refusal, a controlled dawn collecting exactly one, a broke dawn collecting nothing with the debt holding, the final morning clearing the ledger with the loan counted, the goal value at two, the ledger riding the save, legacy migration), audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~AOW-34 · Age of War: the War Loan~~** ✅ — the credit round marches on the wall, and it is the purest tempo trade in the whole arc: **from Age II, 400 gold NOW (L, or the new 🏦 Loan button beside Bond) against 500 of the field's FUTURE coins — the lender's men stand at the coin pile and take every second coin until the ledger clears**, principal plus a 25% vig. The seam is one `garnishCoins(n)` function wired into BOTH routes gold takes off the field — the manual coin tap in `collectCoinsNear` and the 🧲 Lodestone's auto-collect — so the debt is repaid by the same combat income every other system reads, and the relic that makes coins leap to your purse makes the lender's cut leap with them. Borrowed tempo is the whole point: a Ballista bought a wave early kills enough to pay its own vig, which is exactly the trade the round teaches. One loan at a time; a run that ends mid-debt takes the ledger down with it (reset clears `loan` + `loansTaken/loansCleared/loanRepaid`); the HUD button counts down the debt live (`430g owed`); 🏆 **Leveraged** crowns two cleared loans in one run. Verified one-shot via a temporary hook (10 assertions: the button beside Bond, the Age-I refusal + 400/500 constants, a signed loan wiring exactly 400 with 500 booked, the one-loan refusal mid-debt, the garnish taking exactly every second coin (100 collected → 50 kept, 50 repaid), the final coins clearing capped at the debt with the clear counted, free coins passing whole after, the two-clear crown, a fresh run wiping the ledger), `node --check` clean before and after the strip, audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~LAB-32 · Grow Op: the Pre-Sale~~** ✅ — the credit round reaches the corner, and the Distributor's van learns to pay for product that doesn't exist yet. **A new 📤 button on the fixed row (Act II) pre-sells the NEXT FIVE BAGS off the line at 0.85× today's list — the whole advance wired at signing, the per-bag price locked to that moment's market.** The mechanical heart is `preSaleIntercept(n)`: a single function wired into all THREE routes product takes to the shelf (the player's trim-table haul, the player's cook-station haul, and the Runner's stash run) that lets **the van feed first — owed bags come off the line before they ever touch the stash**, so the pre-sale is genuinely a sale of future production, never a disguised instant-sell of shelf stock. The skill is market timing against the street's live demand drift: sign at a surge peak and you banked it at 0.85× of the top; sign before a surge and you sold the upside for cash in hand — the same read the Chemist's Notebook and the demand ticker already teach. One order at a time; `preSaleOwed`/`preSales` join the explicit save lists with migration ("older lines sold nothing forward"); 🏆 **Sold Forward** crowns four completed orders. Verified one-shot via a temporary hook (11 assertions: the 5/0.85 constants + registration, the garage-hidden button, an advance paying exactly round(list×0.85)×5 with 5 booked owed, the one-order refusal, a 3-bag deposit riding the van whole with the shelf never moving, the next 4 splitting 2-to-van/2-to-shelf with the completion counted, clean deposits reaching the shelf whole once nothing is owed, the four-completion crown, the owed ledger riding the save, legacy migration), hook stripped, audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~TYC-34 · Startup Tycoon: the Factoring Desk~~** ✅ — **Round Twenty-Three opens: the credit round** — after architecture, stored value, wagers and insurance, the arc's fifth instrument is the advance against your own future. The Tycoon leg is revenue factoring: post-IPO, a 🏦 chip beside the hedge's **sells the NEXT 60 SECONDS of receivables for cash now at 0.9× the current run rate — the advance locks at signing, wired instantly**. The mechanical heart is a three-line seam inside `addRevenue`: while the window is open **the books still book — the goal bar, the race, and the analyst all read revenue, not cash — but every receivable lands in the desk's pocket instead of yours**, so factoring never sabotages the race it's priced against. The skill expression is timing: the quote reads the CURRENT rate, so signing before a quiet minute (a retreat emptying the office, a pager window you expect to blow) beats the desk at its own trade, and signing before a keynote glow hands them the edge — the settlement toast prints the ledger either way (`advance − diverted`). One window at a time; an OPEN window rides its own `factor` SAVE_SCHEMA entry (advance, clock, and recouped-so-far all survive reload); `factorsTaken`/`factorWins`/`factorProfit` book the lifetime record; 🏆 **Beat the Desk** crowns three winning windows. Verified one-shot via a temporary hook (11 assertions: the 60/0.9/300 constants + registration, the garage-hidden chip, a funded advance wiring exactly the quote with the 60s window open, the one-window gate, the diversion booking the goal bar +100 while the wallet moves $0 — atomically — a quiet window settling +300 with the win counted, a hot window settling −400 with no win, the three-win crown, an open window + book riding the save, legacy migration), hook stripped, audit at its 12/32 baseline.
- **~~SITE · Round Twenty-Two wrap: the insurance round ships + eureka-v30~~** ✅ — the studio closes its twenty-second flagship round, **the insurance round — the wager round's mirror**: six opt-in premiums, one per game, each priced against the game's own signature disaster (a lost race, a sting, a bled wall, a sweep's confiscation, a waylaid caravan, a landed crow peck), each resolving inside the disaster's own existing code path with no side machinery, and each priced by the same thesis — **the games already sell prevention; this round sells the payout for the day prevention misses**. Patch notes lead with **Round Twenty-Two** — 🛟 Hedge, ⚖️ Lawyer, 🛡️ Rebuilder's Bond, 🚙 Marisol's Garage, 🐴 Guild Surety, 🛟 Assessor's Policy — `SW_VERSION` rolls to **eureka-v30** so installed PWAs pick the whole round up, the `patchnotes` suite tracks the new lead, and all six README flagship blurbs weave in their insurance clause.
